Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I really appreciate it. Um, I hope I'm in the right place, and I don't want to waste your time, but... well, I came in because I've been having this feeling. 

It's probably nothing major, but I just feel like I can't quite settle inside my own skin anymore. Even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still and resting, I get this constant urge like I have to jump right up and do the next thing. I hope that doesn't sound too silly or strange... is that the sort of thing you can help with?

Oh, dear. Um... I'm a little embarrassed to admit this, because I really try my best to be polite and respectful. I never want to be a bother to anyone. But... I suppose I do have a bit of a hard time just staying put.

And I end up shifting around in the chair quite a bit. I try so hard to hide it, but sometimes the ladies beside me start looking over at me, and I just feel terrible. I really don't mean to be a nuisance or disrespectful. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information... I know it's probably just a silly habit.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I really hate to complain, and I know everyone has their struggles, but... yes, I suppose it is getting a bit difficult. I feel awful even saying it, because I should be able to handle these things at my age. 

But, um... my family has actually had to start stepping in to help me with my bills and paperwork. I just couldn't seem to keep it all straight, no matter how hard I tried. And... well, at church and my garden club—groups I just love so dearly—I've sort of dropped the ball a few times. Other people have had to step in and redo plans or cover responsibilities I was supposed to handle. 

I just feel so embarrassed about it. It breaks my heart to think that the people I care about might feel like they can't count on me anymore. I really try to be helpful, I promise I do. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or careless... do you think that's something that can happen to people like me?

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... well, I suppose I was always a little bit of a handful, though I promise I never meant to be naughty. I actually... well, I remember my old report cards from back in grade school. The teachers were always very sweet, and they usually wrote that I was bright, which was nice of them to say. 

But... they also wrote that I could be a bit careless. I was always losing my papers, and sometimes I'd accidentally talk out of turn when I was supposed to be quiet and listening. And they did have to remind me quite a bit to just stay in my seat and finish my schoolwork. I always felt so terrible for making them tell me twice, I really did want to be a good student. 

Does that help answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information... I hope I don't sound like I was a terrible child.

Oh... well, um, I wouldn't say I have *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I always have the best of intentions! But... I suppose sometimes things don't always get finished exactly how they were supposed to be.

Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there.

Oh, well... goodness, thinking back to when I was a little girl... I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. But... well, I suppose I did get a little bit overexcited sometimes.

Even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still and resting, I get this constant urge like I have to jump right up and do the next thing. I hope that doesn't sound too silly or strange... is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or ideas that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I don't think so? I don't get stuck on thoughts like that, at least not in a way that bothers me. Sometimes I might worry a little bit about getting things done or if I'm doing things right, but... that's just normal, isn't it? I hope I'm answering that correctly. I just don't want to make a big deal out of anything that's just, you know, a normal part of getting older.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still in situations where you're expected to remain seated?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear. Um... I'm a little embarrassed to admit this, because I really try my best to be polite and respectful. I never want to be a bother to anyone. But... I suppose I do have a bit of a hard time just staying put. \n\nLike, well, even in church meetings—where I know I'm supposed to be perfectly still and paying attention—I'll catch myself tapping my foot, or just sort of rubbing the edge of my purse over and over. And I end up shifting around in the chair quite a bit. I try so hard to hide it, but sometimes the ladies beside me start looking over at me, and I just feel terrible. I really don't mean to be a nuisance or disrespectful. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information... I know it's probably just a silly habit.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings and behaviors causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really hate to complain, and I know everyone has their struggles, but... yes, I suppose it is getting a bit difficult. I feel awful even saying it, because I should be able to handle these things at my age. \n\nBut, um... my family has actually had to start stepping in to help me with my bills and paperwork. I just couldn't seem to keep it all straight, no matter how hard I tried. And... well, at church and my garden club—groups I just love so dearly—I've sort of dropped the ball a few times. Other people have had to step in and redo plans or cover responsibilities I was supposed to handle. \n\nI just feel so embarrassed about it. It breaks my heart to think that the people I care about might feel like they can't count on me anymore. I really try to be helpful, I promise I do. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or careless... do you think that's something that can happen to people like me?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty sitting still when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I suppose I was always a little bit of a handful, though I promise I never meant to be naughty. I actually... well, I remember my old report cards from back in grade school. The teachers were always very sweet, and they usually wrote that I was bright, which was nice of them to say. \n\nBut... they also wrote that I could be a bit careless. I was always losing my papers, and sometimes I'd accidentally talk out of turn when I was supposed to be quiet and listening. And they did have to remind me quite a bit to just stay in my seat and finish my schoolwork. I always felt so terrible for making them tell me twice, I really did want to be a good student. \n\nDoes that help answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information... I hope I don't sound like I was a terrible child.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly get a little flustered when I make a mistake or let my friends down, like I was saying before... but just sitting around worrying about things? No, I wouldn't say I do that. \n\nI always try my very best to just look on the bright side and take things one day at a time. I really do try to be a positive person for the people around me. Is... is that okay? I hope that's a helpful answer. I just wouldn't want to say yes and make you think I'm a nervous wreck when I'm really not. Am I doing alright with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring behaviors or rituals that you feel compelled to perform?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no... I don't think I do anything like that. I mean, I have little habits, I suppose, like everyone does, but nothing I feel... forced to do. \n\nI try to keep things tidy, but I certainly don't have any strict rituals or anything of that sort. I just go about my day as best I can. I really hope I'm understanding what you're asking... I wouldn't want to mislead you. Did I answer that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, sometimes I might make a little decision on the spur of the moment, like buying a special treat at the grocery store or deciding to call a friend just to say hello. But... acting without thinking at all? No, I always try to be mindful of how my actions might affect others. \n\nI really do try my best to be considerate and thoughtful. I would hate to do something rash that would cause a problem or make someone upset. I... I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were wondering about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with completing tasks or following through on instructions at home or in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, um, I wouldn't say I have *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I always have the best of intentions! But... I suppose sometimes things don't always get finished exactly how they were supposed to be. \n\nLike with my quilting charity... if one of the ladies gives me three or four things to do, I'm always so eager to help. I'll start the very first one right away, I really will! But then... oh, I don't know, I might wander off into another chore, like sorting the fabric scraps or making a pot of tea for everyone, and... well, the last steps of what she asked me to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do. I don't want them to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about the charity. I try so hard to be helpful. Is... is that what you mean? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy, because I'm always busy doing *something*, I promise! Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there. \n\nBut then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing, but just hearing a car door shut outside, or seeing my phone light up out of the corner of my eye, or... or even just having a sudden thought about what I need to weed in the garden... it just pulls me completely off track. \n\nI always mean to go right back to the paperwork, I really do! I just sort of... lose my place and wander off to do something else. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly scatterbrained. I just notice so many things going on around me, and my mind just flits away. Is... is that what you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of examples.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupting others when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, thinking back to when I was a little girl... I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with completing tasks or following through on instructions at home or in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, um, I wouldn't say I have *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I always have the best of intentions! But... I suppose sometimes things don't always get finished exactly how they were supposed to be. \n\nLike with my quilting charity... if one of the ladies gives me three or four things to do, I'm always so eager to help. I'll start the very first one right away, I really will! But then... oh, I don't know, I might wander off into another chore, like sorting the fabric scraps or making a pot of tea for everyone, and... well, the last steps of what she asked me to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do. I don't want them to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about the charity. I try so hard to be helpful. Is... is that what you mean? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy, because I'm always busy doing *something*, I promise! Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there. \n\nBut then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing, but just hearing a car door shut outside, or seeing my phone light up out of the corner of my eye, or... or even just having a sudden thought about what I need to weed in the garden... it just pulls me completely off track. \n\nI always mean to go right back to the paperwork, I really do! I just sort of... lose my place and wander off to do something else. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly scatterbrained. I just notice so many things going on around me, and my mind just flits away. Is... is that what you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of examples.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupting others when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, thinking back to when I was a little girl... I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. But... well, I suppose I did get a little bit overexcited sometimes. \n\nI remember my teachers back in grade school... they always wrote on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was so nice of them to say! But they would also write that I could be a bit... careless, I think was the word. I was always losing my papers, and... oh, it's terrible to admit, but I did have a habit of talking out of turn. I just had so many thoughts in my head, and I wanted to share them right away! And they did have to remind me quite a bit to stay in my seat or to finish my schoolwork. \n\nI didn't mean to be disruptive, I promise. I just... I had a hard time waiting, I suppose. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a naughty child! I just had a lot of energy and enthusiasm. Is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often forget appointments, deadlines, or important dates?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Daily_Activities_Forgetfulness\nmanifestation: \"I forget ordinary things over and over, like returning my daughter’s call, paying a bill before the notice turns red, or bringing the folder I promised for a meeting.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... well, I suppose I do have a little trouble with my memory sometimes. Just like anyone else my age, I imagine! But... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I don't want you to think I'm irresponsible, because I really do try my absolute best to keep on top of things. 

But I do seem to forget just... ordinary things, over and over again. Like, if my daughter calls, I'll tell myself to call her right back, but then the day gets away from me and it just completely slips my mind. Or... well, sometimes I might forget to pay a bill until the notice turns red in the mail, which makes me feel just awful. And just the other day I promised to bring a specific folder to one of my meetings, and I walked right out the door without it. 

I feel so terrible when it happens, I really do! I have the best intentions, I promise. I try to write things down so I don't let people down. I hope you don't think terribly of me for being so forgetful. Does... does that answer your question alright? I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.

Oh, well... goodness, that's a very thoughtful question. I suppose it's a bit of both, really? I certainly don't mean to leave things unfinished, I promise you that! I always start out with such good intentions, and I really do want to be helpful. 

But... well, for example, I volunteer with a lovely quilting charity—we make blankets for people who need them, and it's such wonderful work. But sometimes, if someone gives me, say, three or four things to do for the group... oh, it sounds dreadful to say it out loud, but I might start the first task right away, full of energy! And then... I don't know, I just sort of wander into another chore, maybe sorting some fabric or something else entirely, and... well, the last steps of what I was supposed to do just never seem to get done. 

I feel just awful about it when I realize! It's not that I don't want to finish, or that I'm trying to be difficult. I just... I get sidetracked and lose my way before I can follow through to the end. I hope the ladies at the charity don't think I'm unreliable, because I really do care so much about the work we do. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it properly for you.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... well, that's another very good question. I suppose... well, sitting still isn't quite as hard as it used to be when I was a little girl, though I do still like to keep my hands busy! But... oh, I suppose the paying attention part is really where I struggle the most. 

I feel so silly admitting this, because I really do try to be a good reader and stay informed. But, for instance, when I sit down with the senior center newsletter, or even just the schedule for my choir... I'll start out just fine! I really will. I want to know what's going on. But then... well, after just a few minutes, I'll realize my eyes have just been sort of... skimming over the same lines, over and over again. And I'll stop and think, "Goodness, I have absolutely no idea what I just read!" 

It's so frustrating, and I feel just terrible when I miss an important announcement because of it. I don't want anyone to think I don't care about the choir or the center, because I do, very much! I just... my mind seems to drift off, even when I'm trying my very hardest to focus. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Is that what you meant?

I hope that doesn't sound too silly or strange... is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or ideas that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. Sometimes I might worry a little bit about getting things done or if I'm doing things right, but... that's just normal, isn't it? I hope I'm answering that correctly. I just don't want to make a big deal out of anything that's just, you know, a normal part of getting older.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still in situations where you're expected to remain seated?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear. Um... I'm a little embarrassed to admit this, because I really try my best to be polite and respectful. I never want to be a bother to anyone. But... I know it's probably just a silly habit.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings and behaviors causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really hate to complain, and I know everyone has their struggles, but... yes, I suppose it is getting a bit difficult. I just couldn't seem to keep it all straight, no matter how hard I tried. And... well, at church and my garden club—groups I just love so dearly—I've sort of dropped the ball a few times. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or careless... do you think that's something that can happen to people like me?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty sitting still when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I suppose I was always a little bit of a handful, though I promise I never meant to be naughty. And they did have to remind me quite a bit to just stay in my seat and finish my schoolwork. I always felt so terrible for making them tell me twice, I really did want to be a good student. \n\nDoes that help answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information... I hope I don't sound like I was a terrible child.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly get a little flustered when I make a mistake or let my friends down, like I was saying before... but just sitting around worrying about things? No, I wouldn't say I do that. \n\nI always try my very best to just look on the bright side and take things one day at a time. I really do try to be a positive person for the people around me. Is... is that okay? I hope that's a helpful answer. I just wouldn't want to say yes and make you think I'm a nervous wreck when I'm really not. Am I doing alright with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring behaviors or rituals that you feel compelled to perform?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no... I don't think I do anything like that. I mean, I have little habits, I suppose, like everyone does, but nothing I feel... forced to do. \n\nI try to keep things tidy, but I certainly don't have any strict rituals or anything of that sort. I just go about my day as best I can. I really hope I'm understanding what you're asking... I wouldn't want to mislead you. Did I answer that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, sometimes I might make a little decision on the spur of the moment, like buying a special treat at the grocery store or deciding to call a friend just to say hello. But... acting without thinking at all? No, I always try to be mindful of how my actions might affect others. \n\nI really do try my best to be considerate and thoughtful. I would hate to do something rash that would cause a problem or make someone upset. I... I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were wondering about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with completing tasks or following through on instructions at home or in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, um, I wouldn't say I have *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I always have the best of intentions! But... I suppose sometimes things don't always get finished exactly how they were supposed to be. \n\nLike with my quilting charity... if one of the ladies gives me three or four things to do, I'm always so eager to help. I'll start the very first one right away, I really will! But then... oh, I don't know, I might wander off into another chore, like sorting the fabric scraps or making a pot of tea for everyone, and... well, the last steps of what she asked me to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do. I don't want them to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about the charity. I try so hard to be helpful. Is... is that what you mean? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy, because I'm always busy doing *something*, I promise! Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there. \n\nBut then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing, but just hearing a car door shut outside, or seeing my phone light up out of the corner of my eye, or... or even just having a sudden thought about what I need to weed in the garden... it just pulls me completely off track. \n\nI always mean to go right back to the paperwork, I really do! I just sort of... lose my place and wander off to do something else. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly scatterbrained. I just notice so many things going on around me, and my mind just flits away. Is... is that what you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of examples.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupting others when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, thinking back to when I was a little girl... I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. But... well, I suppose I did get a little bit overexcited sometimes. \n\nI remember my teachers back in grade school... they always wrote on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was so nice of them to say! Or... well, sometimes I might forget to pay a bill until the notice turns red in the mail, which makes me feel just awful. And just the other day I promised to bring a specific folder to one of my meetings, and I walked right out the door without it. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do! I hope I'm explaining it properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with sitting still or with paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, that's another very good question. Just like anyone else my age, I imagine! But... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I don't want you to think I'm irresponsible, because I really do try my absolute best to keep on top of things. \n\nBut I do seem to forget just... ordinary things, over and over again. Like, if my daughter calls, I'll tell myself to call her right back, but then the day gets away from me and it just completely slips my mind. Or... well, sometimes I might forget to pay a bill until the notice turns red in the mail, which makes me feel just awful. And just the other day I promised to bring a specific folder to one of my meetings, and I walked right out the door without it. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do! I have the best intentions, I promise. I try to write things down so I don't let people down. I hope you don't think terribly of me for being so forgetful. Does... does that answer your question alright? I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with staying on task and completing things is more because you get distracted or because you just can't seem to sit still and focus?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, that's a very thoughtful question. I suppose it's a bit of both, really? I certainly don't mean to leave things unfinished, I promise you that! I always start out with such good intentions, and I really do want to be helpful. \n\nBut... well, for example, I volunteer with a lovely quilting charity—we make blankets for people who need them, and it's such wonderful work. But sometimes, if someone gives me, say, three or four things to do for the group... oh, it sounds dreadful to say it out loud, but I might start the first task right away, full of energy! And then... I don't know, I just sort of wander into another chore, maybe sorting some fabric or something else entirely, and... well, the last steps of what I was supposed to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel just awful about it when I realize! It's not that I don't want to finish, or that I'm trying to be difficult. I just... I get sidetracked and lose my way before I can follow through to the end. I hope the ladies at the charity don't think I'm unreliable, because I really do care so much about the work we do. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with sitting still or with paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, that's another very good question. I suppose... well, sitting still isn't quite as hard as it used to be when I was a little girl, though I do still like to keep my hands busy! But... oh, I suppose the paying attention part is really where I struggle the most. \n\nI feel so silly admitting this, because I really do try to be a good reader and stay informed. But, for instance, when I sit down with the senior center newsletter, or even just the schedule for my choir... I'll start out just fine! I really will. I want to know what's going on. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Oh, my... well, going all the way back to when I was a little girl? That's... oh dear, it's a bit embarrassing to think about, to be completely honest. I don't want to paint myself as a troublemaker, because I really was a sweet child, I promise! I always wanted to please my teachers. 

But... well, yes, I suppose it has been going on for a very, very long time. Even back in grade school, I remember... oh, my poor mother, she used to get so frustrated with me. My teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was lovely of them to say, but... they also said I was a bit careless. I was always losing my school papers, or... or talking out of turn when I was just so excited to share something. And they were always having to remind me to stay in my seat and finish my assignments. 

I feel so dreadful just saying it out loud! It sounds like I was terribly naughty, but I really didn't mean to be. I just couldn't seem to help it, no matter how hard I tried to be good. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for how I was as a child. Do... do those old school memories help you at all with what you need to know?

I would hate to do something rash that would cause a problem or make someone upset. I... I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were wondering about? I don't know, I just sort of wander into another chore, maybe sorting some fabric or something else entirely, and... well, the last steps of what I was supposed to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel just awful about it when I realize! It's not that I don't want to finish, or that I'm trying to be difficult. I just... I get sidetracked and lose my way before I can follow through to the end. I hope the ladies at the charity don't think I'm unreliable, because I really do care so much about the work we do. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with sitting still or with paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, that's another very good question. I suppose... well, sitting still isn't quite as hard as it used to be when I was a little girl, though I do still like to keep my hands busy! But... oh, I suppose the paying attention part is really where I struggle the most. \n\nI feel so silly admitting this, because I really do try to be a good reader and stay informed. But, for instance, when I sit down with the senior center newsletter, or even just the schedule for my choir... I'll start out just fine! I really will. I want to know what's going on. But then... well, after just a few minutes, I'll realize my eyes have just been sort of... skimming over the same lines, over and over again. And I'll stop and think, \"Goodness, I have absolutely no idea what I just read!\" \n\nIt's so frustrating, and I feel just terrible when I miss an important announcement because of it. I don't want anyone to think I don't care about the choir or the center, because I do, very much! I just... my mind seems to drift off, even when I'm trying my very hardest to focus. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, or completing tasks for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, going all the way back to when I was a little girl? That's... oh dear, it's a bit embarrassing to think about, to be completely honest. I don't want to paint myself as a troublemaker, because I really was a sweet child, I promise! I always wanted to please my teachers. \n\nBut... well, yes, I suppose it has been going on for a very, very long time. Even back in grade school, I remember... oh, my poor mother, she used to get so frustrated with me. My teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was lovely of them to say, but... they also said I was a bit careless. I was always losing my school papers, or... or talking out of turn when I was just so excited to share something. And they were always having to remind me to stay in my seat and finish my assignments. \n\nI feel so dreadful just saying it out loud! It sounds like I was terribly naughty, but I really didn't mean to be. I just couldn't seem to help it, no matter how hard I tried to be good. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for how I was as a child. Do... do those old school memories help you at all with what you need to know?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your difficulty with completing tasks is more because you have trouble paying attention or because you have too much energy and can't sit still?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Constant_Activity\nmanifestation: \"Most days I move from the garden to the senior center to church errands to sorting papers at home without taking a real breath, as if stopping would make everything fall apart.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... well, you're asking such wonderful questions, really helping me think about things. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... well, I suppose it's a bit of both, still? But maybe... maybe the energy part is a bigger piece of it than I'd like to admit. 

I don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I am so blessed to be able to stay active at my age! Some people just sit in front of the television all day, and I certainly don't do that. I try to be very productive. But... oh dear, it's just that most days, I find myself rushing from the garden, straight over to the senior center, then running errands for the church, and then coming home to sort papers... all without really taking a breath. 

It's silly, I know, but I almost feel like... well, like if I stop moving, everything will just fall apart. I just keep going and going. I try to channel it all into helping others, I really do! I just hope it doesn't seem like I'm too scattered or... or frantic. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about? I hope that's a helpful example for you.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I really appreciate it. I just don't want to make a big deal out of anything that's just, you know, a normal part of getting older.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still in situations where you're expected to remain seated?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear. I just go about my day as best I can. I really hope I'm understanding what you're asking... I wouldn't want to mislead you. Did I answer that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, sometimes I might make a little decision on the spur of the moment, like buying a special treat at the grocery store or deciding to call a friend just to say hello. But... acting without thinking at all? No, I always try to be mindful of how my actions might affect others. \n\nI really do try my best to be considerate and thoughtful. I would hate to do something rash that would cause a problem or make someone upset. I... I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were wondering about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with completing tasks or following through on instructions at home or in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, um, I wouldn't say I have *trouble*, exactly. I don't want them to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about the charity. I try so hard to be helpful. Is... is that what you mean? Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there. \n\nBut then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing, but just hearing a car door shut outside, or seeing my phone light up out of the corner of my eye, or... or even just having a sudden thought about what I need to weed in the garden... it just pulls me completely off track. \n\nI always mean to go right back to the paperwork, I really do! I just notice so many things going on around me, and my mind just flits away. Is... is that what you were asking about? I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. But... well, I suppose I did get a little bit overexcited sometimes. \n\nI remember my teachers back in grade school... they always wrote on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was so nice of them to say! But they would also write that I could be a bit... careless, I think was the word. I was always losing my papers, and... oh, it's terrible to admit, but I did have a habit of talking out of turn. I just had so many thoughts in my head, and I wanted to share them right away! And they did have to remind me quite a bit to stay in my seat or to finish my schoolwork. \n\nI didn't mean to be disruptive, I promise. I just... I had a hard time waiting, I suppose. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a naughty child! I just had a lot of energy and enthusiasm. Is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget appointments, deadlines, or important dates?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I suppose I do have a little trouble with my memory sometimes. Just like anyone else my age, I imagine! But... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I don't want you to think I'm irresponsible, because I really do try my absolute best to keep on top of things. \n\nBut I do seem to forget just... ordinary things, over and over again. Like, if my daughter calls, I'll tell myself to call her right back, but then the day gets away from me and it just completely slips my mind. Or... well, sometimes I might forget to pay a bill until the notice turns red in the mail, which makes me feel just awful. And just the other day I promised to bring a specific folder to one of my meetings, and I walked right out the door without it. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do! But then... oh, I don't know, I might wander off into another chore, like sorting the fabric scraps or making a pot of tea for everyone, and... well, the last steps of what she asked me to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do. I don't want them to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about the charity. I try so hard to be helpful. Is... is that what you mean? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy, because I'm always busy doing *something*, I promise! Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there. \n\nBut then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing, but just hearing a car door shut outside, or seeing my phone light up out of the corner of my eye, or... or even just having a sudden thought about what I need to weed in the garden... it just pulls me completely off track. \n\nI always mean to go right back to the paperwork, I really do! I just sort of... lose my place and wander off to do something else. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly scatterbrained. I just notice so many things going on around me, and my mind just flits away. Is... is that what you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of examples.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupting others when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, thinking back to when I was a little girl... I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. But... well, I suppose I did get a little bit overexcited sometimes. \n\nI remember my teachers back in grade school... they always wrote on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was so nice of them to say! But they would also write that I could be a bit... careless, I think was the word. I was always losing my papers, and... oh, it's terrible to admit, but I did have a habit of talking out of turn. I just had so many thoughts in my head, and I wanted to share them right away! And they did have to remind me quite a bit to stay in my seat or to finish my schoolwork. \n\nI didn't mean to be disruptive, I promise. I just... I had a hard time waiting, I suppose. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a naughty child! I just had a lot of energy and enthusiasm. Is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget appointments, deadlines, or important dates?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I suppose I do have a little trouble with my memory sometimes. Just like anyone else my age, I imagine! But... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I don't want you to think I'm irresponsible, because I really do try my absolute best to keep on top of things. \n\nBut I do seem to forget just... ordinary things, over and over again. Like, if my daughter calls, I'll tell myself to call her right back, but then the day gets away from me and it just completely slips my mind. Or... well, sometimes I might forget to pay a bill until the notice turns red in the mail, which makes me feel just awful. And just the other day I promised to bring a specific folder to one of my meetings, and I walked right out the door without it. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do! I have the best intentions, I promise. I try to write things down so I don't let people down. I hope you don't think terribly of me for being so forgetful. Oh, dear... well, that is a tough one to admit. I feel just terrible even talking about it, because good manners are so very important to me. I really do try my absolute hardest to be polite and listen well! 

But... oh, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, yes, I do interrupt people more than I mean to. It happens quite a bit during our committee meetings at the church and the senior center. Someone else will be right in the middle of talking, and suddenly a thought will just pop into my head, and... well, I just blurt it right out before I can stop myself! 

I never, ever mean to be rude, I promise! It's just that I get so worried I'll completely lose the thought if I don't say it right that very second. I always apologize profusely the moment I realize what I've done. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly ill-mannered or selfish? I just get so enthusiastic, and my brain seems to move a bit too fast for my filter sometimes. Is that... is that the kind of information you need?

I suppose I do have a bit of a hard time just staying put. \n\nLike, well, even in church meetings—where I know I'm supposed to be perfectly still and paying attention—I'll catch myself tapping my foot, or just sort of rubbing the edge of my purse over and over. And I end up shifting around in the chair quite a bit. I try so hard to hide it, but sometimes the ladies beside me start looking over at me, and I just feel terrible. I really don't mean to be a nuisance or disrespectful. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information... I know it's probably just a silly habit.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings and behaviors causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really hate to complain, and I know everyone has their struggles, but... yes, I suppose it is getting a bit difficult. I feel awful even saying it, because I should be able to handle these things at my age. \n\nBut, um... my family has actually had to start stepping in to help me with my bills and paperwork. I just couldn't seem to keep it all straight, no matter how hard I tried. And... well, at church and my garden club—groups I just love so dearly—I've sort of dropped the ball a few times. Other people have had to step in and redo plans or cover responsibilities I was supposed to handle. \n\nI just feel so embarrassed about it. It breaks my heart to think that the people I care about might feel like they can't count on me anymore. I really try to be helpful, I promise I do. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or careless... do you think that's something that can happen to people like me?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty sitting still when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, I suppose I was always a little bit of a handful, though I promise I never meant to be naughty. I actually... well, I remember my old report cards from back in grade school. The teachers were always very sweet, and they usually wrote that I was bright, which was nice of them to say. \n\nBut... they also wrote that I could be a bit careless. I was always losing my papers, and sometimes I'd accidentally talk out of turn when I was supposed to be quiet and listening. And they did have to remind me quite a bit to just stay in my seat and finish my schoolwork. I always felt so terrible for making them tell me twice, I really did want to be a good student. \n\nDoes that help answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information... I hope I don't sound like I was a terrible child.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly get a little flustered when I make a mistake or let my friends down, like I was saying before... but just sitting around worrying about things? No, I wouldn't say I do that. \n\nI always try my very best to just look on the bright side and take things one day at a time. I really do try to be a positive person for the people around me. Is... is that okay? I hope that's a helpful answer. I just wouldn't want to say yes and make you think I'm a nervous wreck when I'm really not. Am I doing alright with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring behaviors or rituals that you feel compelled to perform?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no... I don't think I do anything like that. I mean, I have little habits, I suppose, like everyone does, but nothing I feel... forced to do. \n\nI try to keep things tidy, but I certainly don't have any strict rituals or anything of that sort. I just go about my day as best I can. I really hope I'm understanding what you're asking... I wouldn't want to mislead you. Did I answer that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, sometimes I might make a little decision on the spur of the moment, like buying a special treat at the grocery store or deciding to call a friend just to say hello. But... acting without thinking at all? No, I always try to be mindful of how my actions might affect others. \n\nI really do try my best to be considerate and thoughtful. I would hate to do something rash that would cause a problem or make someone upset. I... I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were wondering about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with completing tasks or following through on instructions at home or in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, um, I wouldn't say I have *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I always have the best of intentions! But... I suppose sometimes things don't always get finished exactly how they were supposed to be. \n\nLike with my quilting charity... if one of the ladies gives me three or four things to do, I'm always so eager to help. I'll start the very first one right away, I really will! But then... oh, I don't know, I might wander off into another chore, like sorting the fabric scraps or making a pot of tea for everyone, and... well, the last steps of what she asked me to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do. I don't want them to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about the charity. I try so hard to be helpful. Is... is that what you mean? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy, because I'm always busy doing *something*, I promise! Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I suppose I do get a little bit sidetracked sometimes. I really try my hardest to concentrate, I promise I do! But... well, for instance, when I sit down at the kitchen table to sort the bills or do my paperwork, I have every intention of finishing it right then and there. \n\nBut then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing, but just hearing a car door shut outside, or seeing my phone light up out of the corner of my eye, or... or even just having a sudden thought about what I need to weed in the garden... it just pulls me completely off track. \n\nI always mean to go right back to the paperwork, I really do! I just sort of... lose my place and wander off to do something else. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly scatterbrained. I just notice so many things going on around me, and my mind just flits away. Is... is that what you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of examples.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupting others when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, thinking back to when I was a little girl... I was always a good child, I really was! I wanted so much to please my teachers. But... well, I suppose I did get a little bit overexcited sometimes. \n\nI remember my teachers back in grade school... they always wrote on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was so nice of them to say! But they would also write that I could be a bit... careless, I think was the word. I was always losing my papers, and... oh, it's terrible to admit, but I did have a habit of talking out of turn. I just had so many thoughts in my head, and I wanted to share them right away! And they did have to remind me quite a bit to stay in my seat or to finish my schoolwork. \n\nI didn't mean to be disruptive, I promise. I just... I had a hard time waiting, I suppose. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a naughty child! I just had a lot of energy and enthusiasm. Is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget appointments, deadlines, or important dates?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I suppose I do have a little trouble with my memory sometimes. Just like anyone else my age, I imagine! But... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I don't want you to think I'm irresponsible, because I really do try my absolute best to keep on top of things. \n\nBut I do seem to forget just... ordinary things, over and over again. Like, if my daughter calls, I'll tell myself to call her right back, but then the day gets away from me and it just completely slips my mind. Or... well, sometimes I might forget to pay a bill until the notice turns red in the mail, which makes me feel just awful. And just the other day I promised to bring a specific folder to one of my meetings, and I walked right out the door without it. \n\nI feel so terrible when it happens, I really do! I have the best intentions, I promise. I try to write things down so I don't let people down. I hope you don't think terribly of me for being so forgetful. Does... does that answer your question alright? I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with staying on task and completing things is more because you get distracted or because you just can't seem to sit still and focus?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, that's a very thoughtful question. I suppose it's a bit of both, really? I certainly don't mean to leave things unfinished, I promise you that! I always start out with such good intentions, and I really do want to be helpful. \n\nBut... well, for example, I volunteer with a lovely quilting charity—we make blankets for people who need them, and it's such wonderful work. But sometimes, if someone gives me, say, three or four things to do for the group... oh, it sounds dreadful to say it out loud, but I might start the first task right away, full of energy! And then... I don't know, I just sort of wander into another chore, maybe sorting some fabric or something else entirely, and... well, the last steps of what I was supposed to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel just awful about it when I realize! It's not that I don't want to finish, or that I'm trying to be difficult. I just... I get sidetracked and lose my way before I can follow through to the end. I hope the ladies at the charity don't think I'm unreliable, because I really do care so much about the work we do. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with sitting still or with paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, that's another very good question. I suppose... well, sitting still isn't quite as hard as it used to be when I was a little girl, though I do still like to keep my hands busy! But... oh, I suppose the paying attention part is really where I struggle the most. \n\nI feel so silly admitting this, because I really do try to be a good reader and stay informed. But, for instance, when I sit down with the senior center newsletter, or even just the schedule for my choir... I'll start out just fine! I really will. I want to know what's going on. But then... well, after just a few minutes, I'll realize my eyes have just been sort of... skimming over the same lines, over and over again. And I'll stop and think, \"Goodness, I have absolutely no idea what I just read!\" \n\nIt's so frustrating, and I feel just terrible when I miss an important announcement because of it. I don't want anyone to think I don't care about the choir or the center, because I do, very much! I just... my mind seems to drift off, even when I'm trying my very hardest to focus. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, or completing tasks for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, going all the way back to when I was a little girl? That's... oh dear, it's a bit embarrassing to think about, to be completely honest. I don't want to paint myself as a troublemaker, because I really was a sweet child, I promise! I always wanted to please my teachers. \n\nBut... well, yes, I suppose it has been going on for a very, very long time. Even back in grade school, I remember... oh, my poor mother, she used to get so frustrated with me. My teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was lovely of them to say, but... they also said I was a bit careless. I was always losing my school papers, or... or talking out of turn when I was just so excited to share something. And they were always having to remind me to stay in my seat and finish my assignments. \n\nI feel so dreadful just saying it out loud! It sounds like I was terribly naughty, but I really didn't mean to be. I just couldn't seem to help it, no matter how hard I tried to be good. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for how I was as a child. Do... do those old school memories help you at all with what you need to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with completing tasks is more because you have trouble paying attention or because you have too much energy and can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, you're asking such wonderful questions, really helping me think about things. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... well, I suppose it's a bit of both, still? But maybe... maybe the energy part is a bigger piece of it than I'd like to admit. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I am so blessed to be able to stay active at my age! Some people just sit in front of the television all day, and I certainly don't do that. I try to be very productive. But... oh dear, it's just that most days, I find myself rushing from the garden, straight over to the senior center, then running errands for the church, and then coming home to sort papers... all without really taking a breath. \n\nIt's silly, I know, but I almost feel like... well, like if I stop moving, everything will just fall apart. I just keep going and going. I try to channel it all into helping others, I really do! I just hope it doesn't seem like I'm too scattered or... or frantic. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about? I hope that's a helpful example for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, that is a tough one to admit. I feel just terrible even talking about it, because good manners are so very important to me. I really do try my absolute hardest to be polite and listen well! \n\nBut... oh, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, yes, I do interrupt people more than I mean to. It happens quite a bit during our committee meetings at the church and the senior center. Someone else will be right in the middle of talking, and suddenly a thought will just pop into my head, and... well, I just blurt it right out before I can stop myself! \n\nI never, ever mean to be rude, I promise! It's just that I get so worried I'll completely lose the thought if I don't say it right that very second. I always apologize profusely the moment I realize what I've done. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly ill-mannered or selfish? I just get so enthusiastic, and my brain seems to move a bit too fast for my filter sometimes. Is that... is that the kind of information you need?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I have the best intentions, I promise. I try to write things down so I don't let people down. I hope you don't think terribly of me for being so forgetful. Does... does that answer your question alright? I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with staying on task and completing things is more because you get distracted or because you just can't seem to sit still and focus?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... goodness, that's a very thoughtful question. I suppose it's a bit of both, really? I certainly don't mean to leave things unfinished, I promise you that! I always start out with such good intentions, and I really do want to be helpful. \n\nBut... well, for example, I volunteer with a lovely quilting charity—we make blankets for people who need them, and it's such wonderful work. But sometimes, if someone gives me, say, three or four things to do for the group... oh, it sounds dreadful to say it out loud, but I might start the first task right away, full of energy! And then... I don't know, I just sort of wander into another chore, maybe sorting some fabric or something else entirely, and... well, the last steps of what I was supposed to do just never seem to get done. \n\nI feel just awful about it when I realize! It's not that I don't want to finish, or that I'm trying to be difficult. I just... I get sidetracked and lose my way before I can follow through to the end. I hope the ladies at the charity don't think I'm unreliable, because I really do care so much about the work we do. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with sitting still or with paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, that's another very good question. I suppose... well, sitting still isn't quite as hard as it used to be when I was a little girl, though I do still like to keep my hands busy! But... oh, I suppose the paying attention part is really where I struggle the most. \n\nI feel so silly admitting this, because I really do try to be a good reader and stay informed. But, for instance, when I sit down with the senior center newsletter, or even just the schedule for my choir... I'll start out just fine! I really will. I want to know what's going on. But then... well, after just a few minutes, I'll realize my eyes have just been sort of... skimming over the same lines, over and over again. And I'll stop and think, \"Goodness, I have absolutely no idea what I just read!\" \n\nIt's so frustrating, and I feel just terrible when I miss an important announcement because of it. I don't want anyone to think I don't care about the choir or the center, because I do, very much! I just... my mind seems to drift off, even when I'm trying my very hardest to focus. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with restlessness, fidgeting, or completing tasks for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, going all the way back to when I was a little girl? That's... oh dear, it's a bit embarrassing to think about, to be completely honest. I don't want to paint myself as a troublemaker, because I really was a sweet child, I promise! I always wanted to please my teachers. \n\nBut... well, yes, I suppose it has been going on for a very, very long time. Even back in grade school, I remember... oh, my poor mother, she used to get so frustrated with me. My teachers would write on my report cards that I was a bright girl, which was lovely of them to say, but... they also said I was a bit careless. I was always losing my school papers, or... or talking out of turn when I was just so excited to share something. And they were always having to remind me to stay in my seat and finish my assignments. \n\nI feel so dreadful just saying it out loud! It sounds like I was terribly naughty, but I really didn't mean to be. I just couldn't seem to help it, no matter how hard I tried to be good. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for how I was as a child. Do... do those old school memories help you at all with what you need to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with completing tasks is more because you have trouble paying attention or because you have too much energy and can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, you're asking such wonderful questions, really helping me think about things. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... well, I suppose it's a bit of both, still? But maybe... maybe the energy part is a bigger piece of it than I'd like to admit. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I am so blessed to be able to stay active at my age! Some people just sit in front of the television all day, and I certainly don't do that. I try to be very productive. But... oh dear, it's just that most days, I find myself rushing from the garden, straight over to the senior center, then running errands for the church, and then coming home to sort papers... all without really taking a breath. \n\nIt's silly, I know, but I almost feel like... well, like if I stop moving, everything will just fall apart. I just keep going and going. I try to channel it all into helping others, I really do! I just hope it doesn't seem like I'm too scattered or... or frantic. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about? I hope that's a helpful example for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, that is a tough one to admit. I feel just terrible even talking about it, because good manners are so very important to me. I really do try my absolute hardest to be polite and listen well! \n\nBut... oh, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, yes, I do interrupt people more than I mean to. It happens quite a bit during our committee meetings at the church and the senior center. Someone else will be right in the middle of talking, and suddenly a thought will just pop into my head, and... well, I just blurt it right out before I can stop myself! \n\nI never, ever mean to be rude, I promise! It's just that I get so worried I'll completely lose the thought if I don't say it right that very second. I always apologize profusely the moment I realize what I've done. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly ill-mannered or selfish? I just get so enthusiastic, and my brain seems to move a bit too fast for my filter sometimes. Is that... is that the kind of information you need?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without fully considering the potential consequences of your actions?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... well, that's a very direct question, isn't it? I... I'm not entirely sure how to answer that one. I really try to be thoughtful about the things I do, especially at my age. You learn to be a bit more careful, I suppose. \n\nI don't think I do things without thinking about what might happen... at least, not in a big, reckless way! I would never want to do anything dangerous or... or upsetting to anyone. I try to make good choices, I really do. \n\nIf I do act quickly, it's usually just because I'm trying to get things done, you know? Like rushing to get the church bulletin finished or deciding to reorganize the pantry on a whim. But I wouldn't call that acting on impulse without thinking of the consequences... would you? I hope I'm understanding your question correctly. I just don't think I have a big problem with that sort of thing. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh, goodness... well, you really do have a way of getting right to the heart of the matter, don't you? I so appreciate how thorough you're being with me. 

I suppose... well, if I really think about it, the reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone is... it's the restlessness. Oh, dear... well... yes, I suppose so. I mean, I really don't want to make it sound like I've been a mess my whole life, because I've always tried my very best to be helpful and organized! But... if I'm looking back... yes, it's always been there.
